ParlaMint-AT_1996-02-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00008_d7e2486	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! High house! As hardly any other area of civil law, rent law is characterised by a particular social component. This is simply and simply due to the fact that it is a basic need for man, namely to live on whose satisfaction people are existentially dependent. Therefore, they cannot react simply to a shortage of good housing or to an extravagant price level as in other non-consumable goods, which is why the market forces' play in this area can only be limited. Its resulting social function is fully in line with the existing rental law and the resulting Judicatur as a whole. ==References== Despite the objections expressed here at the time of its creation and already well-known, housing rights change law has made a number of important improvements, which can also be attributed to a social dimension. First of all, the most extensive reduction in the so-called six-month contracts that allowed rents to be completely outside the scope of the rental law is to be mentioned here. In conjunction with often difficult chain lease contracts, this type of contract has often been used in the past to rob the most socially vulnerable to the full extent of the legal protection of tenants. By limiting the six-month contracts, a very sensitive gap in the protection of tenants could be concluded. The same applies, without being able to address it in detail, to the legal elimination of the previous one-year contracts by the 3. Law on housing rights. The certainly still-optimising target value system has by no means missed certain positive effects on the rental interest rate level. It should not be overlooked that the category A housing area is 3rd. The law on the right of residence is practically subject to a rent-rate scheme. This has led to a very noticeable reduction in the rental interest level for these objects. This, as well as some other restrictions on the interest rate of housing, is accompanied by a further development, which entails a tangible relief for those seeking housing, namely the clear decline in the unscrupulous redemption. ==References====External links== Thus, the law on housing rights has succeeded in effectively opposing the forbidden redundancies. ==References====External links== Law on housing rights, important improvements in the field of conservation and improvement: On the one hand, contributions previously withdrawn but not yet used could at last be added to their actual provision, namely the maintenance of the houses, on the other hand, by increasing the amount of funds collected for maintenance, the number of rental interest increases which were very taxed on the tenants under § 18 of the Law on Rental Law has been significantly reduced. This also made a contribution to the approximation of different rental charges in one and the same house. Of course, the observation of rental law also shows areas in which problems exist, which also have a social dimension. These are mainly the time-limits for rental contracts relating to apartments and the rental interest for business premises. ==References== Residential change law created a general possibility to limit leases to exactly three years of legal effect, has made use of numerous landlords. ==References====External links== However, it should be feared that many families who have only received temporary rental contracts under the new legal situation will lose their residence in 1997 because the landlord will not agree to an extension because it could only take place for an indefinite period. Since I felt that an arbitrary intervention by the legislator to convert such leases into lease contracts was very problematic for an indefinite period of time, I advocated the creation of legal measures for an agreed, temporary extension of these leases. However, caution and reluctance should be exercised in this context in order not to push back the number of leases concluded for an indefinite period of time even further than before. This could not be without an impact on the effectiveness of legal tenant protection, because it is unlikely that a tenant hoping to extend the contract would hardly want to engage in a serious discussion with the landlord. Another word about business rental: ==References== Law on housing rights has been intervened in existing rental contracts for business premises insofar as in many cases – for example, in the case of a change of power in the company that owns the business venue – the landlord has been able to raise the rental interest in 15-year-olds to a reasonable rental rate. Many landlords, however, have demanded very high rent rates – that is, inappropriate – as part of this increase. Of course, these do not have to be paid by the tenant. The tenant may carry out a judicial review of the demand for increase. It has the option of reimbursing the reasonable interest rate specified by the court, which is determined taking into account the possibilities of yield in the industry, until the termination of the first instance, based on non-payment of the raised interest rate, is completed, thus preventing termination. Since relevant decisions of the Supreme Court concerning the appropriateness and also industry relevance or experience as set out in § 46a of the Law on Rentals are not yet known, it is not yet possible to state whether a legislative measure is required to amend § 46a of the Law on Rentals. Ladies and gentlemen! In particular these, but also a number of other questions, I believe that a broad public debate is needed, which I also wanted to give an impetus to with my previous words. The Federal Ministry of Justice will continue to actively participate in this necessary discussion process. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-02-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00008_d7e2583	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The issue of "living" is undoubtedly one of the most sensitive social policy issues – the Federal Minister has said – and the fair distribution of housing according to individual needs and demands requires a constant observation of this area. In this respect, the issue of "living" is always a current topic for us Social Democrats, and we do not only deal with it when, for example, a current hour is held here. One of the biggest challenges in housing policy is to resist the temptation to rest on the successes of the past. Even if this is always denied by the FPÖ: These successes are undisputed. Since the First Republic, it has laid the foundations for our exemplary housing policy by developing a global housing policy and policy that is still recognized today. This has continued continuously in the Second Republic, in particular in the areas of housing promotion, housing public utility law and tenant protection. Although Austrian housing policy is extremely good at international comparisons, critical comments must also be made. However, this must be done punctually and purposefully. A flat-rate rejection, as said by my previous speaker by the Freedom Party, and evasion of the ordinary system, in my opinion, is simply without responsibility, because obviously only personal, political populist calculations are assumed. ==References== Many things were achieved in the field of fixed-value rents, as, contrary to the previous legal status, a limit on category A rents was reached again. Furthermore, there was a reduction in the short-term non-social lease contracts and, also mentioned by the Federal Minister, an increase in the protection of sub-rents as well as an improved possibility to hold back unauthorised redundancies; also the enforcement of necessary maintenance work on the part of the tenant was clearly facilitated. However, the S & D Party has always stressed that the 3rd party is a member of the European Parliament. The law on housing rights is a compromise and not the complete implementation of social-democratic ideas in the housing sector. We have therefore long sought meaningful adjustments. We have, in particular, warned against the insufficient determination of surcharges and discounts from the outset. We also consider it possible to extend the scope of an comprehensible rental rate limitation – for example, on sponsored dwellings. In addition, there is a need to further reduce the time-limit, and we are also open to a change in individual regulatory competences. In all this, however, it must be stressed that compromises will also be on the agenda in the home area, as a housing law reform will remain without broad social consensus or without a compromise commitment to consistent enforcement and control otherwise grey legal theory. In this legislative period, we will therefore make every effort to ensure that people who need an apartment receive decent and, above all, affordable apartments. We will work to ensure that we continue to discuss the possibility of finding clearer rental rates or restrictions. This means further development of the indicative system with clearly limited surcharges and discounts, as well as a widest limitation of time-limitation, and this does not mean short-term lease contracts as far as possible. We thus strengthen the protection of termination and do something against housing on time. We also call for an extension of the scope of the rental right to dwellings, particularly in terms of price formation. We want to strengthen the development of housing, as the consolidation programme already provides, and we need to use funding more efficiently and socially accurately. I would like to say to Mr Krüger, after he has gone to seminars to study the new rental right: I think it would also have been useful – you were not in the process of negotiations at the time – to look at the proposals of the liberals. For all these proposals, which were made to us by the Freedom Party, which at the time you were not in the process of the negotiations, would not only have led to significant increases for all tenants. I don't wonder, because in your ranks the landlords are just sitting: the real estate agents. That's nothing negative, they have the right to make money. You only want this at the expense of the tenants. We are here to protect the tenants and to ensure that social balance is maintained in the future. This has been written to you, Mr Krüger, in the main book. - Thank you very much.

November, this European Convention on the Prevention of Torture and Inhuman or Humiliating Treatment or Punishment has been signed in Strasbourg; we call it the Convention on Torture. This Convention has been one of the Council of Europe and is still one of its conceptions. That is to say, members of this Convention have, of course, only been able to be Member States of the Council of Europe. Now these two additional protocols, which are available for adoption today, have provided the opportunity, ladies and gentlemen, for States which are not members of the Council of Europe to join and be able to send members to the Committee and for the possibility of the Convention to be extended to those non-European countries, which are not members of the Council of Europe. Examples of this are the United States and Israel, which are in cooperation with the Council of Europe, but also others. This, ladies and gentlemen, is another step that has been taken internationally and can continue to be taken in the future, namely pressure, moral pressure – I say that quite soberly, because real pressure cannot be exerted in any other way – by inviting States to join and then subject themselves to this agreement. This is, I believe, a very important and very good thing, and I am very glad that Austria will now ratify these protocols. We're not under the first, but we're in the front. Peter Schieder, you shake the head – I know we're already relatively late, but it's not so many before us yet, and I think it's very important that we don't go after that and the last or last ones. That is why I am very pleased that we are going to decide unanimously today. To Protocol No 2 I can take a very short look. It is essentially a provision of content which allows the choice of committee members to be re-elected twice. A term of office shall be four years, i.e. by virtue of these provisions, it may be that a member of the Committee shall exercise this function for twelve years. I believe that this is not bad, because it produces a certain continuity of those who act, and I therefore believe that it is very correct, very important and very good for this Austrian Parliament – and also with regard to our reputation in the Council of Europe – that we will decide unanimously today on these two proposals. Thank you.

I would like to wish the new member of the Council of Europe, the Russian Federation, who is now solemnly elected, good cooperation and a good future in the common house of Europe.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-02-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00008_d7e4567	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! The additional protocols available to you for the adoption of the decision are not content but organisational in nature, but they are very visible and impressive about the extension of the work of the International Committee set up under the Convention. It states that the idea of preventive international control of the conditions of withdrawal measures of any kind has prevailed. In particular, I would like to draw attention to the fact that the development of a European standard for detention and detention conditions by a respected international body effectively complements such control facilities as they exist at national, national level, for example, in accordance with § 18 of the Austrian Criminal Enforcement Act by the establishment of enforcement committees. As mentioned today, the Committee set up under the Convention paid a second visit to Austria in the autumn of 1994 and took into account a number of detention facilities, both inside and outside the area of justice. The report, which was then adopted by the Committee and by Mrs Stoisits, was quite comprehensive and confidential after its introduction to Austria. A detailed opinion of the two sectors concerned is before completion. In the sense of the procedure followed after the Committee's first visit to Austria, it is intended to agree to the joint publication of the report and the Austrian opinions. The Committee and Austria will then agree to a publication together. Until then, he's confidential. However, I believe that the publication will be expected in the near future.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-02-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00008_d7e4958	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I have already deposited in committee that it is never too late for the performance of homework. In this sense, we have in principle welcomed the fact that the Novelle Copyright Act was finally discussed in the Committee and passed on to the Assembly for the vote. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! I am saying this about the performance of the homework because there has been a considerable delay in the law. I would remind you that already in the Declaration of Government of the XVIII. Legislative period agreed that copyright is to be adapted in a timely manner. In 1994, at the end of the legislative period, the Novelle Copyright Act was finally to be adopted in the Committee and sent to the National Council for the vote. At the time, however, there was a blockade, a blockade because the various stakeholders, but also the ORF as a producer, could not agree with the filmmakers on the division of remuneration claims. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! I should like to remind you that the Copyright Act provides for a presumption of law in the film works, which means that, regardless of copyright production, all rights of exploitation come to the producer. Therefore, for a very long time, it has been a legitimate concern of the film-makers that, in view of the remuneration claims after the empty-case remuneration and cable television, a change is taking place in favour of the film-making place. We are pleased to note that there has finally been an amendment here. As a cultural speaker, I also see it as my task, Mr Cap, to work for the filmmakers, for the film owners. Unfortunately, God’s reward for film-makers was blocked for years by the ORF, but also by the great coalition. Ladies and gentlemen! There has then been an agreement. There is now a 50-to-50 division with regard to the remuneration claims according to the empty-case remuneration and after cable television. However, there is still this legal presumption that all rights of exploitation are granted to the producer. Now, as I think it is right, the film-makers are on the point of view that the remuneration, the empty cassettes and cables are not at all a case of this presumption of law, because the law says that the rights of exploitation are to the film producers; on the other hand, the claims for compensation are not the case. From this point of view, from the point of view of the film-makers, it was only too understandable that the film-makers had spoken of expropriation, which was the result of these remuneration claims, and that, in the end, there was no satisfaction with this division among film-makers. But especially for this reason, there is no satisfaction with the filmmakers, because they are the authors of a film work. This is also clear and clearly stated here. Well, the producer has the economic risk, but it's the directors, it's the Cutter, the Dialogue Directors and so on, who carry the authorship. Consensus, which was finally reached, is therefore unsatisfactory because it does not concern the old films or only insufficiently. I should like to remind you that it was originally planned that film-makers should also be involved in the remuneration claims concerning the old films of 50 to 50 percent with the film producers. But then the Austrian radio has introduced its veto and only wanted a regulation pro futuro, i.e. only for new films. Finally, a regulation has been adopted in the form of no remuneration at all for the films filmed before 1970, and for the films that were filmed between 1970 and 1. 4. In 1996, a reasonable remuneration is provided for. Well, we see this as an insufficient compromise, because it is partly a expropriation of the authorship of the film-makers, who are supposed to benefit from these new remuneration claims. For, ladies and gentlemen, one could naturally object: In 1970, the producer of film carried this economic risk, and why should the film-makers now be distributed accordingly? But I would remind you that, in accordance with our copyright law and under the law, it is not so long ago that there is a cable remuneration at all, and therefore it would only be right and cheap from our point of view if the filmmakers, the film owners, were also involved in the old films. Thus, ladies and gentlemen – but the ladies and gentlemen of the great coalition have to answer – this delay has resulted in enormous damage to the film-makers. I would remind you that 30 million Schillings were taken each year from this remuneration already mentioned several times, which only the producers have received. That is, if you use this 50 to 50 division, 15 million schilling have been missed each year by the film maker through this delay. Ladies and gentlemen! That was also the reason why in the past legislative period – I think it was in June – we made a corresponding deadline request. It was really no longer to be seen that the filmmakers should continue to abandon their due tantimes. Ladies and gentlemen! Although this division is a compromise control in the final effect, but it benefits the film-makers, one thing must be noted: The text of the law contains the passage that this division takes place only if nothing else is agreed. One can now assume from the imbalance, the professionalism of the producers and the imbalance with the professions who are creative in the production of a film work that genuine partnership does not exist, because, of course, the film producers are far superior to the film-makers in professionalism, business ability, order conditions, contract and commitment conditions. That is why I would consider it – that is my personal opinion – to be good if you consider this right – that is not the claim of honour in the film contract, but only the claims of compensation – to be normative, because you can be sure, ladies and gentlemen, that the film manufacturers will, of course, change their general terms of order soon, so that the film-makers will not be able to do anything, unless they are really well-known directors. We liberals, or at least part of the liberals, may I say, therefore take the view that this 50-to-50-regulatory right should be mandatory. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! In the field of translations and the editing of film works, the position of the authors has worsened. This must also be said clearly and clearly, because the copyright law novelle is sold by red and black under the pre-text of an improvement in the legal status of the film owners, and there are certainly provisions which do not only mean better creation, but even deterioration. And that's what the translations are all about. Here it is added that the film owners are no longer to be asked in the editing of films and in the translations, and these translation rights should then only be given to the film producers. I have already commented on the transitional provisions. We liberals have already tabled an amendment to the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection in order to include old films in this remuneration in favour of film-makers, and we will also be tabling an amendment here in plenary. Ladies and gentlemen! However, there is now a very different problem at the centre of public interest than that of film-makers, namely the problem of so-called copy tax. According to the amendment of the Copyright Act, a so-called copy tax is to be lifted, i.e. the completeness of which is to say that it is not a tax in the sense of tax collection of the federal or state, but a compensation which is intended to benefit the authors. And, ladies and gentlemen, how to turn it and turn it, it doesn't matter: It is clear that this copy tax – whether called the reprography fee or copy tax now – entails a further, very serious burden on the economy. It is even a double tax provided for in this law, and that is quite easy to reject. We have a completely different system remuneration here. So every manufacturer of copiers who sells copiers on the market has to pay a corresponding charge. Of course, this charge is charged at the prices of the copying equipment. As the initial calculations have already shown, it is expected that when the novels are adopted in this form, all of them – please – will become more expensive by 10 percent. And I'm wondering, Where is the representation of the economy in the Austrian People's Party, which can agree to such a scheme? I understand, of course, the authors who want to know their rights. But, please, this regulation is indifferent because it affects all copying devices, no matter who makes use of these copying devices. Whether it is a trader who never intends to copy any works of authors subject to copyright, or whether it is a shopping partnership or a professional profession, no matter what, everyone has indiscriminately here to pay a tax or indirectly to pay the increase in the sales price. I therefore believe that a further burden is imposed on the Austrian economy. I am of the opinion that it would be counterproductive, especially in difficult times, if one were to undifferentify the economy – well aware: undifferentiated – whether copyrights are misused here or not, to consider formal and dogmatically with a tax, with a device remuneration. Ladies and gentlemen! But we liberals are also against the copy tax for another reason. This bill, this wording of the provision of § 42b of the Copyright Law Novelle, is practically 1 to 1 written out from Germany; one could now say, in order to support here the duplication of the events, has been copied. So there is a copy of the German legal situation here to tax the manufacturer of copiers, but also the economy, which lives partly from the copiers and uses them, unilaterally. The fact that the writing down of laws, which are already eight or nine years old, is not always good is also evidenced by the fact that it is subject to a closer review, namely because, although the copyiers are covered, the new technologies are not. For one cannot compare with the knowledge of the year 1996 and today's technology a situation with a situation of interest in 1985 when computers, screens, CD-ROMs and printers who have copied did not yet exist to this extent and this problem was not yet relevant. I therefore note that the same situation would be dealt with differently. Only the copying devices would be discriminated against, but the reproductions by computer screens, CD-ROMs and printers would remain unscathed here. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The fact that this is not fair and that it cannot be possible for all to be shaved over a comb here, whether they copy author's rights here or not, must be clearly established once. I'm sure that's not useful. Or think of an industrial company, my dear friends. Ladies and gentlemen, who never came to mind, intervene in copyrights and copy any technical books that only have to deal with accounting documents at copy level, or are copied of invoices only. All these entrepreneurs are here unreflected in this remuneration. However, I also believe that from the point of view of schools, universities and vocational training institutions, which would then have to pay a second tax, it is not acceptable and responsible at the moment, given the budgetary crisis and the restrictions on research and universities, that there is a double taxation here again. Because, ladies and gentlemen, think of schools. They have to pay for the first time when they buy the copying machine. And the second time, the individual copy will be taxed once again by way of an operator's remuneration, so that according to the current calculations it is expected that not only the copying equipment will become more expensive, but also the individual copy by about 35 sizes per piece. [[President Dr Neisser shall take over the chairmanship.]] Ladies and gentlemen! I cordially invite you to accept our amendment. On the one hand, the amendment provides for an improvement in the legal status of the film-makers, the film-copy, on the one hand, in the view of the old films, which are fully involved, on the other hand, through the full exercise of the remuneration rights, also in the case of translation rights. On the other hand, this amendment also provides for a cancellation of the copy tax for the reasons mentioned. Ladies and gentlemen! I appeal in particular to the Austrian People's Party not to let this law enter into force with the copy tax. I have heard yesterday that the Austrian People's Party is already trying to compensate the economy for such a unilateral provision and supports a longer period of entry into force, but of course that does not benefit. Because once a law has been passed, a later date of entry into force does not change anything at the time of entry into force – unless, ladies and gentlemen of the Austrian People's Party, you want a later entry into force only to be able to prepare another novel. But it really doesn't make sense to pass a law and renovate it in the next breath. Ladies and gentlemen! For the reasons set out above, therefore, we are proposing the amendment. If the amendment is not complied with, we see ourselves forced to reject the whole of the novel at third reading, knowing that with our amendment we would have rightly provided the film maker with a good service according to their work.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-02-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00008_d7e5100	The next item is the speech by Mrs Mag. Dr. Fekter. Please, Madam Member, you have the floor.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-02-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00008_d7e5122	Mr President. Minister! High house! The Novelle Copyright Act 1996 has been intensively consulted for two years, since it has already been updated in 1993. At that time Community law was adapted to the EEA. The wishes of the artists were not sufficiently taken into account in 1993, but it was promised to make a larger novel that is available here today. The aim of the novelle is an appropriate bill for the exploitation of the products of artists, authors, cultural creators. Primaryly, this will improve the economic situation of the cultural maker. That is to say, in addition to the so-called exorbitant subsidies, he has the opportunity to receive a fee for his performance via the recovery contributions. This is just an attempt at equality between material and intellectual property. In other words, in a highly developed economy, in a market economy as we understand ourselves, a market value must be granted in addition to material and intellectual products. But with us, spiritual property is far from the value that should actually come to him. The value added of the copyright industries comprises around 2 per cent of the gross domestic product: These are film, television, video, design, graphics, theater, photography, architecture, advertising et cetera. These sectors offer 74 000 jobs, they are classic growth sectors, and therefore a copyright law novelle is also an economic factor. The aim is to provide fair and fair opportunities in the market to help market mechanisms by means of legal regulations in the form of the granting of legal licences to groups that would otherwise never have their rights. The main point of the novelle is the reprography remuneration mentioned by Mr Krüger, i.e. a remuneration for reproduction – photocopies, other types of reproduction of printing works, including sheet material. This has been carried out in a double-duty manner, namely as a device compensation for the placing on the market of reprography equipment, and as an operator remuneration. It is incorrect, Mr Krüger, if you claim that this will increase the cost of equipment by 10%. Because the equipment on the market is manufactured by international companies, and they can already be offered internationally with this remuneration on the market. That is, the corporations know what it is about, they can deal with it. At present it is true that in Austria, because of – I say so now – a little too little competition these devices are already a little more expensive than abroad. [[Abg. Rossmann: This is because of the higher value added tax in Austria!]] It is not only because of the higher value added tax, Mrs Rossmann, but it is simply so When the market produces it, it is good; if not, if things are bought abroad, the suppliers of this equipment must also address them. [[Abg. Rossmann: High wage side costs! The total cost structure!]] It is true that the cost structure in Austria – as I am on your side – is relatively high, especially in the case of wage costs. But compared to Germany, it is not always necessary that we have higher prices than in Germany. [[Abg. Rossmann: The VAT!]] Apart from VAT. I believe that these devices are not necessarily becoming more expensive. For example, the reprography remuneration in Germany is not 10 percent, as Mr. Krüger has done, but only 0.5 to 2 percent. The law does not contain a percentage, it must be negotiated by the producers and the collecting companies. And in Germany, at present, copies do not become more expensive by 35 sizes – these are figures that are unsanitary. At present, the remuneration price in Germany is between 2 and 4 groschen. I personally am unhappy with this remuneration, I admit. We are thus introducing new bureaucracy, and it is not really perfectly implemented with regard to the technical standard. It is true, however, that in order to protect the intellectual property of authors and composers with a view to weighing the legal interests, the introduction of this remuneration can be accepted. We already know a similar solution when it comes to empty-case remuneration, which works bureaucraticly, although it is unloved in the economy. I do not wish to hide that this tax will entail a significant burden on public budgets. In particular, the educational institutions – school holders, WIFIs, BFIs et cetera – will in future be enormously burdened by this remuneration, especially in the purchase of equipment. If they make copies available for a fee, this fee shall be included in the price and shall be paid to the person who needs the copies. Another focus of this novel is the improvement of the rights of film owners. This is a compromise that allows producers to sell and sell their films worldwide, but also enables film owners – i.e. directors, cameramen, cutters, costume pictures, furniture and cetera – to participate in the economic results of the overall work through further exploitation. The use concerns in particular the new technologies such as cable television, videocassettes et cetera. The distribution of the utilization contributions between producers and film owners in relation to 50 : 50 seems to be a fair solution, balanced between the creative contribution of the authors on the one hand and the capital use and the economic risk of producers on the other. The film actors are not specifically mentioned in these new regulations, but that does not mean that they have no rights. The EU's rental and lending directive expressly recognises the actors. The fact that no tantimes have flown for actors so far is not so much due to the legislation but rather to the efficiency of their own representation to assert these rights of exploitation. It is soon to be expected that film actors will also make a bit of pressure in relation to their collecting society and then share in the revenues. In addition to EU adaptations in the field of satellite and cable TV – satellite transmissions are relevant in the country by copyright, from which the broadcast takes its outcome; I have already mentioned that film-makers are involved in cable recycling rights – the novelle brings a limited exhibition right in the form of a reasonable remuneration claim, an intensification of criminal law regulations when copyrights are breached in commercial terms – i.e. by professionally produced black copies that are brought to the people. The novelle also provides easier access to scientifically protected works for teaching and science, and – this is especially important for Austrian tourism – a legal license for the performance of commercial video cassettes in accommodation establishments. Videocassettes must be provided free of charge. However, the accommodation operator – the hotelier, the host, the pensioner – must, if he offers his guests this ancillary service, pay an appropriate recovery fee to the collecting companies. The video films presented free of charge must be at least two years old, so as to ensure that primary cinema success is not undermined by such performance possibilities. Finally, Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! A major demand of the art-makers, which was made at the conference on copyright in Salzburg in 1992/93, has not been implemented, namely the so-called right to follow-up. As a result, it is about the fact that an artist is to continue to co-participate in the further sale of his work of art. Remember: This is relevant when an artist reaches very high prices for his works in his later stage of work. He should co-participate with the proceeds of his first works, which were still favorable, if they are later sold expensively. In the review procedure, this right of consequence, as not to be expected otherwise, was rejected by the payers – those are the art trades, the galleries et cetera – with the argument that the introduction of the successor right would cause serious economic damage to domestic art trade, which would ultimately also have a negative impact on the artists themselves, since they, if the galleries cannot live, do not receive money. Surprisingly, in the discussion, it was then that well-known artists, especially very prominent painters, joined this argument, but also the Ministry of Economic Affairs and the Ministry of Science, Research and Art, so that no agreement could be reached on it and this right of succession remains in space and for negotiation. At present, there are harmonisation efforts in the EU on copyright. It will be shown whether the reprography compensation or the right to follow will also be standardised by me in an EU directive, and at the latest then, ladies and gentlemen, we will deal with this issue again.

My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! This gap, which strikes in Austria, is really urgent and urgent to close, and in this direction the amendment to be read by the next speaker goes as well. I am saving myself not to repeat the unworthy discussion about a possible postponement of the date of entry into force of the repro-charge. I must say, however, that I am glad that Mrs Fekter and, subsequently, Dr Fuhrmann, who still had this intention until yesterday at this time, have been moved away from it because I can now believe in her perceptiveness that copyright is not about the interests of foreign copiers, but primarily about the protection of the interests of those who are also the object of this novel – and these are artists in Austria and their creations. Ladies and gentlemen! The best-seller paragraph mentioned by Dr Schmidt would have been a particularly great concern to me, precisely because the bestseller paragraph meets a group of writers, namely the youngest or the young, who, if they are surprisingly successful in their work, cannot use it in a material way directly for this work. Perhaps the Federal Minister could take this as a very, very essential suggestion for the next novel of the Copyright Act. There must be a next novel, ladies and gentlemen, because in every discussion relating to the protection of intellectual property and intellectual product, we are going further into a future which cannot be estimated at all. I consider the complex of copyright to be one of the most exciting as a part of the whole of the media right, because we are most rapidly developing, which we are dealing with throughout the civil law field at all. In the case of what we have experienced in recent years alone at the speed of new developments, hardly anyone really keeps pace with the legal regulations. A few years ago, no one knew a CD-ROM. Today, CD-Rom, Internet and the virtual world as a whole are something that almost everyone is confronted with. There is a world in which the legal system and, therefore, the legislator is concerned with legal protection, which, however, is hardly yet tangible and affordable. In particular, ladies and gentlemen of the Ministry of Justice will face a particularly serious challenge in the future, and I hope that we too – in other words, we here in the National Council – will, of course, meet this challenge with their help. In doing so, the European Union, which, by its directives – I believe it is – will always generally be able to give us a very pioneering role, perhaps a small guideline. We can certainly claim to be a pioneer in considering these new regulations if we are to make a timely effort to do so in the future. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] In this sense, the Greens of the Novelles as a whole will agree, although we believe that the next novelle should be negotiated here as soon as possible.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-02-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00008_d7e5911	The Federal Minister for Justice has now spoken. Please, Federal Minister, you have the floor.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-02-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00008_d7e5932	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! I refer briefly to three fundamental aspects of copyright, which are also relevant to this novel, and which have already been addressed today: its importance within the framework of the whole legal order, both as regards the protection of the authors and the interest of the general public in access to copyrighted works or services, as well as the influence of technical development on copyright and, finally, to the relationship with Community law. The fact that copyright is hardly anchored in the general public and only relatively few professionals are familiar is a clear contrast to the actual meaning of copyright both for the cultural and economic life of our country. In addition to the protection of the author's personal relationship to his work from an ideal point of view, the rights of exploitation to the author to safeguard the economic fruits of his intellectual work are of particular practical importance. Only a strong copyright protection makes artistic production possible, which is as independent as possible of public or private measles, and is thus an important element in promoting the cultural performance of a country. It is in the nature of the matter, but does not make the relevant meaning of copyright less significant that its cultural effects are not quantifiable. The economic importance of copyright, on the other hand, can be demonstrated by figures, and the annual value added of this economic sector in Austria by Mrs Fekter is more than 2% of the gross domestic product, expressed in Schilling, at least over 25 billion. Ladies and gentlemen! However, the legislator is not only obliged to adequately protect the interests of the reasonable; it must also take due account of the interests of the general public in access to copyrighted works and services and find a balanced balance between these sometimes contrary interests. I believe that this draft law is not just a matter of obligation, but of satisfactory compromises. A second important aspect is the influence of technological development on copyright today, but the use of its work by the author in most cases by means of technical means is carried out in principle. The Austrian Copyright Act has given impressive confirmation to the great advantage - the case law of the Supreme Court has confirmed this in some cases in recent years - that it is so general and flexible that it can satisfy various technical developments even without the intervention of the legislator. As far as the legislator still has to respond and intervene, it is usually not a question of basic innovations, but of fine-tuning the existing rules, for example by restricting exclusion rights for a particular area to a mere right of compensation or vice versa by restricting previously existing free use of labour to certain cases. The reprography regulation, which has already been discussed several times in this draft law, I believe is a particularly good example. It combines both measures in one way, as I believe, the interests of the mainly Austrian authors on the one hand and the public interest in access to copyrighted works on the other. In connection with technical development, I think a few words on the subject that are currently being discussed not only here in the hall, but all over the world, namely the exploitation of copyright-protected works in data networks or, in order to use a term in the US, in the global information infrastructure. In fact, it is a development that raises multiple copyright issues. In view of the already mentioned openness of the applicable Austrian copyright for new technical exploitation possibilities, it is also important that these questions can first be answered on the basis of the applicable law. I therefore believe that there is no urgent need for legislative action in the other Member States of the European Union, and the effects of the exploitation of copyright-protected works in data networks can be observed in a calm manner at the outset before any urgent legislative action is taken. Finally, a word on the influence of Community law on copyright. It is obvious that this is considerable. The Novelle Copyright Act 1993 has already transposed two relevant directives, and two directives are also implemented with this amendment. The activities of the Community institutions in this area continue: Consider, for example, the considerations about the protection of databases or the harmonisation of the right to follow, as I believe, which is certainly in need of regulation. I believe that this influence of Community law is by no means to be complained of, but that it is essentially a positive view. Copyright-protected works do not in principle have any limits; they are generally used internationally. Despite international agreements in this field, however, each country has its own copyright rules, which are very different not only at the level of protection, but also in various but very important details. This can make exploitation over the national borders extremely difficult, expensive and complicated. The harmonisation of copyright in the Community at a high level of protection, which Austria is already fulfilling, is thus satisfying a real need and will also benefit Austrian authors very much. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-02-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00008_d7e6015	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! This amendment to the Copyright Act is another step towards protecting intellectual property. A number of wishes of the Salzburg Author Congress will be fulfilled with this novel. I can recall that, after this congress, the then cultural speaker of SPÖ and former Minister of Education Hilde Hawlicek called together the art and justice speakers of the parties represented in Parliament and, together with artists, representatives of the collecting societies and other interested parties, discussed the results of this congress, on which we developed first ideas. There was then a first novel, which was of rather limited content – primarily adopted by EU directives at the time – but with the library status a step was taken, for example, which was not undisputed, which also cost the libraries money, but which, on the other hand, took into account the demands of the artists and took a part in the exploitation of their works. The novelle that is present today is, as has been said, a second step. In doing so, further wishes of this author's congress will be realized while at the same time fulfilling the wishes of the users of copyrighted works and services, such as the reprography remuneration, which will be abolished in the form of a device remuneration and operator remuneration. This measure, like most in the field of copyright, is also not undisputed. In the case of reprography remuneration, schools, universities, vocational training institutions or adult education, research institutions, libraries, and so on, additional costs which have not yet been incurred. However, this legalizes the reproduction and use of works, for example in teaching. I think this is a sensible measure. I think that it must be concluded with the actual illegal use of copies in teaching and that it is also in the interests of teachers if there is a clear regulation. But above all, in the field of copyright, we assume that the authors should be properly involved in the economic results of their work. If one is committed to protecting intellectual property, then the associated costs must also be accepted. It has been discussed both in the previous novel and now in the debate about the right of succession. This amendment does not contain any relevant provisions, since the resistance of the various groups was too large. I admit that it is unsatisfying to see that, for example, an art collector can sell an image of an artist who is still young to buy a butterbread and then, if the artist has become famous to sell a very high amount, the artist does not receive anything of it and has nothing of this development. I am very much in favour of promoting young artists, but I am not sure whether copyright is the appropriate way to implement these promotions. Copyright is used for the regulation and design of the exploitation of works of art and not, in fact, the charging of market developments. I think we should think about this question again, and I also recognize that it is really important to promote artists, especially young artists, to give them the opportunity to exhibit, to give them the opportunity to take time for their art. I just don't know if that's the right way. It is clear from copyright that new novels are always needed. Not only on the basis of photocopiers, but also in many other areas, it can be seen that the development of copyright is very much linked to technical development. If there are new developments, it is always necessary to adapt copyright in the interests of producers. Due to the easy copying of texts, works of visual art, computer programs and musical pieces, it was necessary to repeatedly remedy and help the author to his right. How difficult this is can be seen in the field of information technologies – which has already been discussed several times today – and I too would like to focus on this issue now, because I see from the Green Paper of the European Commission and similar discussions that there is a problem that is very difficult to deal with. The networks are already used by the use of digital communication technologies for commercial, educational and research purposes. Networks have essentially developed under open communication standards, and the content exchanged over them is currently only partly protected by intellectual property rights. As the flow of data will increase, its legal and technical protection is of great importance. On the one hand, the use of the data networks should not be impeded, but on the other hand the copyright of intellectual property should be protected. This is also an economic issue because the protection of copyrights has become an essential part of the legal framework necessary for the competitiveness of the cultural industry. Industry will only invest in the various creative activities if it can prevent its illegal exploitation and benefit from the profits of investment. Production and value-added in this area are experiencing very high growth. For example, the audiovisual sector has recorded an annual real growth rate of 6 percent in recent years. The considerable storage capacity for works and data in the field of data-highways covers both traditional works and multimedia art, i.e. a combination of data and works of different nature – image text, music and software. The analysis of the preferred content for the release of CD-ROMS in Europe shows considerable growth in most relevant areas. For example, from 1993 the field of culture and entertainment shows a growth of more than 73 percent, art and age science: 62 percent, advertising, design and marketing: 53 percent, computer programs: 47.8 per cent, and and so on. Although there are only 0.5 percent of private CD-ROM devices in the states of the European Union, in the USA there are already 3.1 percent. That means: The development must not be stopped. However, in response to this development, there should not be any major changes to copyright – the Federal Minister has already mentioned this – but there is no need to take immediate action and take drastic measures. However, it is about adaptations, and therefore the next reform, which is already in view, will have to include the legal protection of databases. There is also a draft directive of the European Union. This means that in the foreseeable future we will have to deal with the issues of copyright again, that we will have to put in place measures that are in line with developments, and that copyright law novels will be necessary in the future. In any case, the innovation now in hand represents a positive step. It is an independent development, not just an EU one. And I think we should continue along this path of consensus, common development, common progress.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-02-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00008_d7e7523	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! A further arch of questions has been asked here. On the one hand, the fact that 31 questions have been listed and a whole package has been added to the list shows the complexity on the one hand, and on the other hand, obviously some of the information residues that are worthwhile in addition to it, because I believe that, of course, an opposition party also has a right to be informed and informed accordingly. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Mr Scheibner! They referred to the changes in the geostrategic situation and the question of Austrian security policy. One can certainly say without exaggeration that the 1989 revolution was not only the biggest change in the geostrategic field of Europe over the past few years and decades, but also probably this century, if not even the last centuries. Europe has lost its role from the main confrontation between East and West. The two hostilely opposed pacts have no longer or are dissolved, the Red Army has undergone a Western-East shift of 1,500 kilometers, and a complete change has taken place as a result of the new set-up in all of Eastern Central Europe. What many experts suspect or have prophesied from the outset, which perhaps was not recognized by a large number of people at first, was the fact that such a change from the outset no longer brings security, but only a shift in security problems, in the form of how experts express it: It was the transition from a "high risk and high stability" situation to a "Lower Risk and Lower Stability" situation, that is, the transition from a situation of high, enormously high risk, but also due to the balance of terror, relatively high stability, a cemetery rest, which was present, to a situation of increased or very high instability, which we all experienced in the example of the outbreak of war in exjugoslavia and an increasing instability in other countries as well. [[Abg. May. Stadler: You don't need to explain this to us!]] [[Abg. May. Stadler: He has such ideas for defense!]] For us, therefore, the question arose from the outset: What is the correct answer to this new situation? – Let me stick to the following. For Europe, it has meant that between the Baltic Sea and the Black Sea a whole series of states have gained their sovereignty, a sovereignty which is very much linked to many instabilitys, because they are states with state borders, some or most of which are not yet 80 years old, because they are states that have relatively little self-confidence and little independent experience, which have not had democratic traditions over the last 50 years, and in which there is, on the other hand, a wealth of political, economic and social problems and, in particular, national problems that cause a high degree of crisis. This means that we have to deal with this problem as a whole. And if we ask ourselves: What possibilities are there to change that? Then one must say that the old recipes are undoubtedly not suitable. According to all probability, there are three possibilities to bring stability to Eastern and Central Europe – theoretically speaking: It is the hegemony of one or more states, it is the balance of forces, and it is integration. For the first time in the history of the continent, there is a chance that this area, from its own, that is not formed by the hegemony of one or more states, can build and achieve an independent order. And that is probably the crucial question for us at the same time. That is, if one wants to prevent individual powers from taking place even in the future, then the first possibility does not come into question, then one must look for other solutions. There is no doubt that a balance of forces cannot be restored because the differences in size are so strong that it is simply impossible to create a balance of power, so that as a real, real third possibility only integration remains. It is therefore also logical that we should make every effort to stabilise this area by means of integration in the field of security policy. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] This also requires, in particular, Austria, a country which has a common border with States that once ruled communistally, to a extent of 1 400 kilometres and which, in total, has four neighbours from this former communist area as the only state in Europe, a contribution to the stabilisation of the path of integration. And it was therefore logical that we should have made new considerations in order to redesign security policy in the future and, in particular, to promote the European path of integration. The solution offered for this is undoubtedly the European Union in the political field. Brussels is the Integration Centre, and there is already a political structure there that could be able to bring together the whole continent in an inclusive way in the future, thereby also overcoming nationalism in security and defence policy as far as possible. On the other hand, we have to say that it is far from so far as it is necessary, of course, to have a high level of conditions. That means it'll take some time. And it is especially necessary to add the security component in the European Union, and this is happening now. As of next month, the 1996 Intergovernmental Conference will be held in which the Member States of the European Union will deal with how in the future a common foreign and security policy can take place, in particular to prevent wars from breaking out in Europe. However, the European Union does not rely on a no-one's country, but, of course, it is based on two organisations which have already provided certain services or which have the necessary conditions to actually bring about this stabilisation: On the one hand, this is the Western European Union, which was hardly a function during the cold war, but in the meantime was defined by the European Union as the security arm, as the defence arm of the European Union, and also as an integral part of the development of the EU in the Maastricht Treaty. That is to say, the security, defence and integration of Europe will or will in future be carried and promoted mainly by the Western European Union, the WEU. It is therefore obvious that this is also a priority for us. [[Abg. Arbitrators: That's not true!]] On the other hand, it is so that... [[Abg. Arbitrators: It's not in there for security policy! It's in there for defence policy, it's not in there, it's security policy!]] I would say, Mr Arbitrator, that a foreign politician like you is aware that security policy is also part of it and cannot be considered in isolation. ParlaMint-AT_1996-02-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00008_d7e8881	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! If, in their urgent request, the ladies and gentlemen of FPÖ speak of a non-functional neutrality and believe that Austria is therefore bound to find the best possible solutions to its security challenges, let me make a few fundamental points. We all know that the security-political landscape of Europe is undoubtedly subject to a dynamic process of change. Of course, the threat of a world war is banned by the disappearance of the East-West confrontation. However, there are a wide range of causes for violent conflicts – starting from classical power policy to the factors in economic, social, ethnic and national areas. And one thing is quite clear: None of the new risks, no matter where their roots lie, concern Austria alone. But any risk can affect our country, and I too believe that these risks can be met better together than alone. Ladies and gentlemen! One thing is also clear: a purely military alliance has long been unable to secure peace and stability. That is why we Socialists are based on a comprehensive concept of security, which goes far beyond the military sphere, which includes, above all, economic, social and environmental conditions. Safety and stability can only be achieved permanently in the interaction of all components. This does not mean, however, that the military dimension of security will lose its importance in the future. On the contrary: The integration of the military area into a new security system is one of the challenges facing a united Europe. Of course, the central problem is European security policy, the question of how we organise the new pan-European security architecture. Federal Minister! If you're talking about full cooperation with NATO, it's called NATO entry – anything else would be fog-throwing. And I can also answer you clearly and clearly: Both NATO and the WEU are not suitable as pure, classical military alliances and, above all, do not intend to take the preventive measures for comprehensive security. The solution is therefore to establish a sustainable pan-European security structure that meets the comprehensive, indivisible and cooperative requirements. In Europe, there is currently a security organization, which includes all 15 EU countries and all other countries, the OSCE. The size of this organization – we know that – is at the same time its weakness. And a reformed OSCE would therefore be a good basis for achieving a pan-European security architecture so that an important first step can be put in the right direction. And as far as our Austrian situation is concerned, there is no compelling security policy reason for Austria to join NATO or the WEU. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] Ladies and gentlemen! Those who stand up for membership of Austria in these military alliances repeatedly justify this with the threats that our country is supposed to face. We believe that in doing so, we operate with deception maneuvers, that there are acts of deception. Because no one here can call us a state that threatens Austria today, and the old friend enemy scenario belongs to history. [[Abg. Scheibner: colleague! Would you have believed in 1990 that a year later we were fighting at our borders?]] Surely that was not to be foreseen. But you cannot derive from it a threat from Austria and reason that it would be important and right to join the WEU and NATO, a classic military alliance of the Cold War. We will certainly not give you any consent to this. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] [[Abg. Scheibner: Can you guarantee this?]] Mr Scheibner and I have repeatedly pointed out here – and I should like to point out, Mr Scheibner, that there is no need, as I have said, to join these military alliances. [[Abg. Scheibner: Can you guarantee this?]] Ladies and gentlemen! In my opinion, entry at the present time would not lead to any more security, but would only reduce our security margin, because it would cover only the military sector at all. We see this in some of the security-related events in Bosnia. The consequences, ladies and gentlemen, would be increased arms expenditure and stationing obligations, which should simply not be forgotten and underestimated. That is why we will not vote in favour of the motion for a resolution on Austria's accession to the WEU. Rather, we welcome the procedure agreed between Federal Chancellors and Vice-Cancellors and will continue this common approach proposed here. Ladies and gentlemen! The peace partnership, on the other hand, is a useful instrument for international cooperation on security policy. We are also participating in this cooperation, and we have therefore clearly and clearly regulated cooperation with the NATO Member States and the other partner countries. [[Abg. Scheibner: colleague! Ask the Tychtl where we sit! We're in the last row!]] It moves in the area of peacekeeping, humanitarian missions and international disaster aid. As far as the WEU is concerned, the debate on its future orientation is still under way in the European Union. The results of the Intergovernmental Conference are waiting for us – and you have said so – to be an equivalent partner and to actively participate, thus bringing in our ideas and conceptual considerations. Ladies and gentlemen! For the Austrian defence policy and the German army, of course, the changed security policy conditions also give rise to changed tasks. Of course, the main task of the German army remains the national territorial defence, but decisive security challenges come to our attention, of course, from the international field in particular. I am thinking, above all, of crisis management and peacekeeping in the framework of the multinational missions on behalf of the United Nations or the OSCE, and of preparatory measures for multinational peace missions in the framework of the Partnership for Peace and future tasks within the framework of the European Union, which may also result from the Intergovernmental Conference. The participation in Bosnia-Herzegovina, ladies and gentlemen, shows that the Austrian German army can handle relatively simple tasks such as the establishment of a transport unit very difficult. And in order for us to respond quickly and effectively to international tasks in the future, we need to increase the availability of associations and units of the federal army to the necessary extent. As far as territorial defence is concerned, the German army – which can be said very well – today is able to fulfill its defence order. And right now – I give many the right to give up military service and neutrality – would be just too absurd against this background. At present, ladies and gentlemen, we are primarily concerned with safeguarding the borders: an order which the German army can actually fulfil and which ensures the satisfaction of our security interests. The security policy and the issues of national defence are two serious issues, which are of course to be dealt with permanently, but they must not be taboo either. In principle, ladies and gentlemen, there is therefore no prohibition of thought and discussion. And that is why the today's unattended, on the other hand, is unacceptable to the Minister of the Interior, which is a little hidden. [[Abg. Dr Puttinger: Are you now in favour of the proposal, or are you against it, Mr Puttinger?]] Ladies and gentlemen! I'll tell you: We Social Democrats confess... [[Abg. Dr. Puttinger: Do you represent the proposal One or not?]] I have not talked about it in terms of content, but I have noted here that there is, in principle, no prohibition on thinking and discussion, no ban on dealing with security issues here. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] That is why we will not agree with the motion for a resolution tabled by colleagues of the Freedom Party against the Minister for the Interior. But I can tell you one thing: We Socialists – and for this I stand – confess to us an efficient and credible national defense! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] For security and democracy reasons there is no alternative to neutrality, but at present no alternative to general military service, because a professional force would not only bring higher costs and less security at the present time, but would raise a number of follow-up problems which have not been observed in the discussion until now and which one has neglected. But we will continue to have to deal with the whole issue very intensively, but, as has been said, in great peace and not in the day-to-day debate. The sensitive area of security policy and national defence is a serious issue and is certainly not suitable, as I have said, for day-to-day conflicts, but we will be very careful, with great thought and peace and responsibility continue to deal with this issue and will contribute to credible national defence.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-02-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00008_d7e9893	The statements by the individual speakers of the political groups have essentially dealt with security policy. Some comments: I believe that understanding NATO or the WEU as a purely military alliance from the cold war is a fundamental mistake. The only fact from which these organizations are composed shows that this is the core and the tribe of the world's oldest and most proven democracies, and its security organization is undoubtedly a model which obviously also has an appeal that goes far beyond that. [[Congratulation with the ÖVP and the Abg. Disc]] For it is no coincidence that from the moment when the pressure in the Eastern Central European countries has been reduced, where the authoritarian communist system has lost its ground, the desire of the population and the rulers has come to these systems after accession – hand in hand with the democratic process. If we look at which of the organizations has the best condition, we must also see that they were all created in the cold war: the OSCE, the NATO, the WEU; some, like the WEU, even have their origins before. It can be said that the OSCE has acquired some merit in reducing prejudices in the cold war, in a situation where the security dialogue had to take place at the conference level. And there is also the main weight. Not for nothing, until a few months ago, the OSCE called "Conference for Security and Cooperation" because it has always presented only one conference, and that will be so in the future. Wherever it is about substance, one must certainly look for other solutions. I believe that this should also be based on the concept of the United Nations. I am reading to you what the Agenda for Peace says, that is, the agenda for the peace of the United Nations. It says no more and no less than: "Imposing peace is beyond the capacity of United Nations exception on a very limited scale." That means making peace is, as it were, a matter beyond the capacity of the United Nations. If you want to make peace, other organizations need it. If, on the other hand, we look at NATO's Statute, we must also acknowledge that it is an organization which, of course, has already been determined by itself in the Statute, that it will act exclusively within the framework of the decisions of the United Nations, and also as long as there is a mandate or until the United Nations intervenes, so that it is an absolutely complementary organization. The second is not just the theory, but the practice. If we look at what has really happened, we must remember that we have discussed this House for hours, which is why the war in Bosnia, in ex-Yugoslavia, will not end. And if we ask ourselves today why, for the first time, there is a situation where there is at least a chance of peace, then the answer is: because NATO – not for itself but for the United Nations – is working there. That is why it is also important to strengthen this process, and in that respect, I believe, we should also remove ourselves much more strongly from the prejudices of the past from the time of the cold war. Perhaps a short word on the further procedure: I believe that it is really unheard of to get into this process as quickly as possible. To wait for a system from the Atlantic to Vladivostok and over to America in 20, in 30 or in 40 years, this will not help us. We now have the instability in the entire eastern half of our continent, which is to be overcome, after this enormous break-up period on the one hand. We can't wait to see if something could emerge from it in 30 or 40 years, but we need a system that can work in a short time. This is the WEU, and that is also NATO. The second is that we probably also have a historic opportunity. I said earlier that the entire Eastern Central European area is always under the hegemony of one or more powers. For the first time in history, there is this opportunity for an order to be created, a self-elected and freely elected order from these states, in cooperation with the Western European states. For the first time in history! These states have always been oppressed, and other orders, foreign orders, have always been imposed on them. I think it is our duty to take advantage of this historic opportunity. We may not have them in ten years, perhaps even in a shorter period of time. That is why we should act now and not miss out on time, because Austria can do so because these areas of conflict and danger are so close to us, because until a short time ago only 150 kilometres away from our state border war has been waged, and because we may have a stronger state of consciousness, perhaps better estimate the situation than countries that are 1,000 or 2,000 kilometres away from each dangerous area. We should take advantage of our opportunity to draw attention to these dangers and also the opportunities to bring peaceful solutions here. I see this as our role, and I see it as the consistent continuation of the former neutrality policy. We are simply trying to bring peace there, we are trying to communicate, we are trying to bring together Europe that has come from different directions. This is the historic task of our country, and we should consider it.

Dr. Ofner]] It is clear to us that the military factor in security policy is no longer dominating, but security policy without defence policy is not possible for us in the longer term. We therefore acknowledge the need for further development of this part and the need to ensure that these tasks can be carried out. However, according to our understanding, the participation of Austrian soldiers in such operations is only possible provided that the task is compatible with the defensive structure of Austrian defence and that the voluntary principle of participation is maintained. The participation of Austria in NATO's Peace Partnership in the areas of peacekeeping, humanitarian action and disaster aid serves to strengthen peace and freedom in the international community. If you produce the popular percentage points, as measured by gross domestic product, we are at 0.8 percent in the last place. I do not make any sense that I would, personally for the security and for the protection of democracy, peace and freedom in our country at the Wehrbudget, would undoubtedly be in favour of a better creation, and would describe the financial framework that we have as a minimum. However, it must not be forgotten that, as an internal country, we are of course very difficult to compare with other countries. [[Abg. Scheibner: With Switzerland!]] Yes, there is Switzerland too, that's right. In addition, the comparison of gross domestic product must be very careful, because, of course, Austria is one of the richest countries despite the urgently needed savings package and therefore a comparison is often very difficult. However, despite the savings price at the Wehrbudget, I would like to point out the considerable improvements in the area of military defence in recent years. With the same resources, a number of modernisation objectives could undoubtedly be achieved. There has been a need for investment through savings in services and posts in central administrations. In addition, it has undoubtedly succeeded in achieving significant improvements for time soldiers, but also an improvement for ground defense workers by increasing compensation by 900 S per month. In my opinion, it is also particularly pleasing – as Mr Murauer and Mr Murauer have already said – that a reasonable solution could be found in civil service, a solution that can be accepted with good conscience – without a conscientious examination, because it then falls away. This is undoubtedly a reasonable solution which is being sought in the field of civil service. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! I was very surprised about these statements, which I would like to say here, of the Minister of the Interior on defence policy, in order to express them very carefully. Yes, I was actually annoyed and certainly cannot share these views. I was contacted in my capacity as an EIA executive officer by many of my colleagues, who have undoubtedly harshly criticized the Minister of the Interior for these theses. These colleagues – be it the Federal Army, the Gendarmerie, or the police – wonder why an Interior Minister argues against security organs, why an Interior Minister questions the existence of the Federal Army and how he can demand the abolition of the general military obligation, so they wonder how an Interior Minister can make such irrantious statements at all. I believe that instead of defaming an entire profession, it would be far more important to take measures against organised crime, because we know that we have an increase of 25 percent there. Urgent need for action – and that seems particularly important to me – is particularly evident in the area of addiction crime. If we look at the 1994 safety report now before us, a further 11 per cent increase in drug victims will have to be observed. We reported 11 percent more drug deaths from 1993 to 1994. . I therefore believe that there is enough scope for the Minister of the Interior for the fight against crime, the fight against addiction, the reform of the Stapo and the like. To defend the country: It is extremely important, in my opinion, that the German army should not be made poor, but that it should be made clear to the German federal army, which was commissioned by the Austrian federal constitution, that the interests of the military state defence are clearly and clearly defined and that, in the coming years, the steps and framework conditions referred to by the Federal Minister for Foreign Affairs, Dr Fassleven, will be taken in the interests of the security of our country.
